# Decisions and alternatives

One row per decision. Ask "why X instead of Y" and the answer should be here.
Status: **proposed** (my pick, not yet confirmed by the owner), **decided**, **open** (waiting on research or the owner).

## Goal (user, 2026-11-18)

An open-source app that gets the most information out of EEG electrodes, to make life better:
sleep tracking, stress / anxiety / focus, "take a break" hints while coding, generative music,
lots of statistics on how things affect the owner. Phone matters at least as much as the PC
(put the headband on, tap one button, sleep). Device order: BrainBit Black first, then the DIY
ADS1299 board (8-16 electrodes, any positions), then others such as OpenBCI.

## Facts measured on this laptop (2026-11-18, Linux Mint 22.3)

- BrainBit Black (firmware 1.10) connects from
  Python with BrainBit's pyneurosdk2 1.0.15: 4 channels O1 O2 T3 T4, 250 Hz, battery read,
  contact check (resistance) works. No pairing needed. No motion sensor or pulse sensor reported.
- Not yet tested on a head: the readings above were with the headband off (contacts = infinity).
- Setup without sudo: `libneurosdk2.so` (from BrainBit's Ubuntu 24.04 package, 1.0.6.39) plus
  libc++ / libc++abi / libunwind 18 unpacked into `~/.local/lib/neurosdk2`; run with
  `LD_LIBRARY_PATH=~/.local/lib/neurosdk2`. Probe: `scripts/brainbit_probe.py`.

## Decisions

**Working rule (owner, 2026-11-18):** the assistant chooses without waiting for the owner and records each choice here with its alternatives, so the owner can steer later. Status: **decided** = in force (by the owner where marked, otherwise by the assistant under this rule; any row can be reopened); **open** = needs a test.
"Switch if" says what would make the alternative the better pick.

| # | Topic | Pick | Status | Alternatives | Why / switch if |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where the code lives | three repos: this one (public app: phone app, shared code, analysis), `eeg_ads1299_lab` (public hardware + build guides), `utof_tracker` (private, imports this app's exports) | decided | one monorepo for app + hardware; EEG features inside utof_tracker | contributors should not need KiCad or hardware safety gates; private data never in a public repo |
| 2 | App framework | Flutter app on Android whose first job is recording all night; Python on the PC for heavy analysis | decided | native Kotlin recorder + Python desktop; Kotlin Multiplatform; React Native; Tauri; Python + browser screens | BrainBit's Flutter package covers Android; the owner already knows Flutter. Recorder shape (docs/research/android-overnight-ble-xiaomi.md): one foreground service of type `connectedDevice` only, never `dataSync` (6 h/day cap on Android 15+); `flutter_foreground_task` 11.0.3 with the whole BrainBit session inside its TaskHandler isolate (the plugin has one instance per engine and starts no service or wakelock itself). Switch to a Kotlin recorder if the 1-hour screen-off test on the Xiaomi loses data; React Native if iPhone becomes the main target |
| 3 | BrainBit connection | BrainBit's NeuroSDK2 (Flutter package on the phone, pyneurosdk2 on the PC), kept in its own module | decided | BrainFlow; raw Bluetooth | only route to the BrainBit Black on every system; BrainFlow has no Black support and no Flutter. Core is a closed binary, hence the separate module. Flutter `neurosdk2` 1.0.21 ships its binaries under its MIT file (docs/research/neurosdk2-flutter.md); the Linux `.so` exports what a Linux desktop plugin would need. On the PC: never import numpy in the recorder process (segfault) |
| 4 | Any electrode positions | each tracker learns its channels from a 1-2 min eyes-closed / eyes-open setup task; positions optional; re-calibrate when electrodes move | decided | require standard positions; large pretrained "brain models" | benchmarks show small classical methods match the big models when data is scarce; pyRiemann is the toolkit |
| 5 | DIY board data format | keep the board's own "E9" format | decided | OpenBCI Cyton mode (about 2-4 days of firmware, estimate); host adapter to LSL | E9 has error checks and sample counters the Cyton format lacks; Cyton 16-channel mode halves the rate. Switch if using OpenBCI's own app becomes important |
| 6 | First trackers | contact check, sleep timing, morning check-in (alpha strength and speed), jaw tension; stress / focus only as the owner's own ratings beside the data | decided | vendor-style stress and focus scores | no brand's stress or focus score has independent validation; these 4 positions cannot measure them |
| 7 | Where signal processing runs | live basics on the phone, everything heavy in Python on the PC | decided | sleep scoring on the phone; shared Rust core; Python on the phone | fastest to build. Switch to on-phone scoring when people without a PC use the app |
| 8 | Sleep model | AnySleep (accepts any channels; code and weights MIT; 128 Hz, robust scaling, no filter), YASA 0.8.1 (BSD-3) on O1-T3 as cross-check; disagreement shown as low confidence | picked, unproven | YASA alone; U-Sleep | published: AnySleep macro-F1 ~0.68 with 1 channel, ~0.73 with 4 (wet PSG); YASA expected ~60-75% on these sites (estimate). Neither was tested on dry side-of-head electrodes: judge on the owner's own nights (docs/research/anysleep.md, yasa-single-occipital.md) |
| 9 | Storage | add-only chunk file with checksums on the phone; the PC joins a night's chunks into one BDF+ (pyedflib), filling missing time with held values marked `BAD_gap`, so file time equals clock time; results as JSON-lines events in utof_tracker's format | decided | write BDF on the phone (needs a Dart writer); keep samples contiguous and only annotate gaps; XDF; HDF5 | a killed app loses seconds, not the night; BDF+ is read by the sleep tools; no Python library writes BDF+D, and sleep epochs must line up with the clock (docs/research/bdf-plus-writing.md) |
| 10 | Sync | app writes to an export folder; Syncthing carries it for the owner; ZIP / USB export for everyone else; "last synced" warning | decided | built-in Wi-Fi transfer; Health Connect | Syncthing already runs for utof_tracker; its Android app changed hands in 2026, so it must not be the only way. Health Connect later as an optional extra: write one multi-stage sleep session per night with `health_connector` (the `health` package cannot), read heart rate / HRV / other apps' sleep (docs/research/health-connect.md) |
| 11 | Sound engine | `flutter_soloud` with rule-based ambient sound; first mode: sound calms as alpha rises, jaw tension blocks rewards | decided | native low-delay audio core; Pure Data; AI-generated music on the PC | low risk, runs on phone and desktop. Switch to a native core only for precisely timed pulses |
| 12 | Statistics | per-day export analysed first by utof_tracker's nightly job; every result labelled "seen together" or "tested"; self-experiments suggested | decided | quick correlations like commercial apps | one person needs about a month of days for a large effect; loose correlations mislead |
| 13 | Break hints | start on the PC from activity data with no headband; add own ratings, then typing rhythm, then the EEG trend; at most 4 a day | decided | EEG-only focus score | no validated EEG break detector exists for these positions |
| 14 | UI | built with the `/impeccable` skill (owner's instruction) | decided | - | user request 2026-11-18 |
| 15 | Licence of the app | MIT | decided (owner) | Apache-2.0; GPL | owner's choice 2026-11-18 |
| 16 | Repository visibility | public on GitHub from the start | decided (owner allowed it) | private until first release | cloud agents and contributors need it; nothing personal is committed (`_local/` is excluded) |
| 17 | PC recorder survives Bluetooth drops | after 40 s without data the recorder process exits and `record_night.sh` starts a fresh one (new file); `to_bdf.py` joins the files of a night | decided | reconnect inside one process | 2026-11-18 night: in-process reconnect after an out-of-range drop segfaulted inside the SDK (exit 139) and StartSignal after a resistance check sent nothing; a fresh process always worked |
| 18 | Brain sound baseline | 60 s calibration at the start (30 s eyes open = 0, 30 s eyes closed = 1, first 5 s of each dropped); if eyes closed gives under 1.5x alpha, or the person skips it, the 10th-90th percentile of the last 5 min (a 2x span centred on it when the range is narrower) | replaced by row 43 (2026-11-18) | running percentile only; fixed µV thresholds; eyes-open level only | calibration gives a scale the person understands ("your eyes-closed level") and doubles as an alpha check; the fallback keeps it working when alpha is weak or the user skips. Switch to running only if real sessions show the calibrated scale drifting off within 10-20 min (contact settling) |
| 19 | Brain sound measures | alpha = log power 8-12 Hz, mean of usable O1/O2; jaw = 30-45 Hz at T3/T4 against its own 20th percentile of 2 min; 1 s Hann window every 0.25 s, alpha smoothed 0.5 s | replaced by rows 42 and 46 (2026-11-18) | relative alpha (alpha / 4-30 Hz); band around each person's alpha peak; 2-4 s windows | short delay matters most for alpha feedback, and absolute alpha is what eyes closing changes most. Switch to relative alpha if real sessions show broad power drifting with contact; to a personal band if the owner's peak sits near 8 or 12 Hz |
| 20 | Brain sound rewards | a bell only after alpha >= 0.7 on the person's scale for 3 s with jaw under 2x rest and a usable side electrode; any block resets the count and locks bells for 2 s; bells exist only as rewards | replaced by rows 44 and 46 (2026-11-18) | Muse-style random birds plus rewards; no rewards, sound only; also block on O1/O2 high-frequency power | muscle can never earn a bell, and every sound you hear maps to a measure. Add an O1/O2 muscle check if real clenches leak into back-of-head alpha |
| 21 | Brain sound prototype | browser page `scripts/web/sound.html` with Web Audio; pure ES modules for features, session, mapping and mixer, tested with `node --test`; engine kept separate | decided | Python audio in the recorder (needs numpy: forbidden there); Tone.js or another audio library; go straight to Flutter | runs today on the laptop with the existing recorder and no install; the pure modules are small enough to port to Dart line by line for `flutter_soloud` (row 11) |
| 22 | Morning check-in protocol | contact check, 2 min eyes closed then 2 min eyes open on a dot, soft tones at each change, then Karolinska Sleepiness Scale (1-9) and mood / tension (1-5); every boundary is a page marker | decided | alternating 1-min closed / open blocks; 3 min each; eyes open first; visual analogue scales | simplest routine that gives the three alpha measures in 5 min; KSS is the standard short sleepiness rating. Switch to alternating blocks if the eyes-closed value drifts within the 2 min (drowsiness turns alpha into theta) or depends on order |
| 23 | Alpha speed (individual alpha frequency) | centre of gravity of the power above the 1/f line in 7-14 Hz, eyes closed, 2 s segments zero-padded to 0.25 Hz; the peak of that excess is stored too; `null` if no point rises 0.1 log units (+26 %) above the line | decided | raw spectral peak; raw centre of gravity (biased low by 1/f); specparam peak; restingIAF (Savitzky-Golay) | a single peak bin jumps with 0.5 Hz resolution and split peaks; removing 1/f first stops the slope from pulling the value down; no extra dependency. Switch to the peak or specparam if it has smaller day-to-day spread on the owner's first two weeks |
| 24 | Artefact rejection for the check-in | 2 s segments, reject if peak-to-peak over 150 µV after a 1-40 Hz band-pass, flat (under 0.5 µV spread) or spread over 3x the phase median; skip the first 3 s and last 1 s of each phase; a channel needs 20 s clean per phase | decided | ICA; autoreject; fixed threshold only | 4 channels are too few for ICA, and autoreject needs many epochs; amplitude rules are simple and explainable. Switch thresholds if real mornings keep under 60 % clean data, or if blinks leak into the eyes-open alpha |
| 25 | 1/f (aperiodic) slope | straight-line fit of log power on log frequency, 2-40 Hz without 7-14 Hz, eyes closed | decided | specparam (FOOOF) | no extra dependency, transparent; beta peaks bias it a little. Switch to specparam if the slope turns out to track anything for the owner |
| 26 | How a check-in is compared | median and range of the last 14 earlier check-ins in the same folder; "within / above / below your range"; a note until 5 exist; never a score or a norm | decided | z-scores; percentiles; population norms | no norms exist for this headband; with under 14 days a z-score is unstable and a range is plain to read. Switch to a rolling percentile once there are 30+ days |
| 27 | Lining up page markers with samples | the marker's sample index `i` (samples received when the marker arrived), not clock time | decided | clock time through the `.idx` arrival times | stays right across Bluetooth gaps and needs no clock maths; delivery delay is about 0.1 s, far under the 3 s skipped at each phase start. Switch if the phone app writes markers without a sample index |
| 28 | Setup test ("what can it see") | 20 cued blinks, 10 left / right glances, 5 jaw clenches, all on tones in about 2 min; each scored as signal-to-noise against the moments around it, "visible" at 2 or more | decided | a free-form recording with hand-labelled events; per-event detection rates | gives a quick yes / no per event type for these 4 sites. Switch to per-event detection once these events drive features (jaw tension, break hints) |
| 29 | Tests without a headband | pytest in `analysis/tests` (run with `uv run --project analysis --with pytest`, so `uv.lock` is unchanged), `node --test` for the page logic (no npm packages); one slow test runs the real recorder on the fake headband | decided | a dev dependency group in `analysis/pyproject.toml`; a JS test framework | nothing new to install or lock; the cloud sessions cannot reach the PyTorch index to re-lock. Switch to a dev group when the lock can be refreshed |
| 30 | Break hints: where activity comes from | ActivityWatch's AFK bucket over its REST API (localhost:5600), polled each minute; the window bucket is not used yet | decided | own idle timer (`xprintidle`, X11 / Wayland idle APIs); keyboard hooks | ActivityWatch already runs on the laptop, keeps history (so `--dry-run` can replay a real day) and works on X11 and Wayland. Switch if ActivityWatch is dropped or its AFK timeout (3 min) proves too coarse |
| 31 | Break hints: what counts as continuous work | AFK gaps under 3 min count as active; 3-5 min gaps pause the count without resetting it; 5+ min resets | decided | treat every gap under 5 min as active; reset on any AFK | short gaps are thinking or reading; a 3-5 min step away is not a real break but also not work. Switch if the owner's ratings show 3-5 min gaps already refresh |
| 32 | Break hints: back-off and snooze | +15 min to the threshold for each hint today that led to no break (snoozed, OK without a break, or no answer); Snooze brings one more hint 20 min later, exempt from the 45 min spacing but not from the cap, quiet hours or a break in between | decided | back off only on Snooze or no answer; snooze also bound by 45 min spacing | an OK with no break is still "not now"; a snooze is the user's own request for a reminder, so spacing (meant against nagging) should not swallow it. Switch if snoozed re-hints feel naggy |
| 33 | Break hints: where state lives | the `break_hint` events file is the only state (read back each minute), a day runs 04:00-04:00 | decided | separate state file; in-memory only | survives restarts and crashes with no second file to keep in sync; the same file feeds later statistics. 04:00 so working past midnight does not reset the daily cap |
| 34 | Break hints: notification | `notify-send -u low --wait --action=...` in a background thread; plain notification if `--action` is missing | decided | D-Bus calls from Python (needs a non-stdlib package); a tray app | standard library only, works on Cinnamon (libnotify 0.8 on Mint 22). Switch to D-Bus if expiry vs close must be told apart |
| 35 | Tests for stdlib-only scripts | plain `pytest` under `analysis/tests/`, importing the script; not added to the analysis uv lock | decided | add pytest to `analysis/pyproject.toml` (lock needs the torch index); unittest | the script and its tests need no numpy or torch, so they run anywhere pytest is. Switch when analysis code gets tests that need the uv environment |
| 36 | Who the app is for (licence consequences) | private use, at most a non-commercial open-source project (owner, 2026-11-18); our own code stays MIT; components with non-commercial terms are allowed (e.g. GEDAI cleaning, SleepFM weights, TimesFM 3.0 weights, BrainFlow's SimpleBLE, flutter_blue_plus free tier) as optional, separately installed parts, each named with its terms in the README | decided (owner) | permissive-only dependencies (needed only for commercial use) | no commercial use is planned. Copying GPL code into this repo would still make shared builds GPL: keep GPL tools (SBaGen, GSSC) as separate programs, not pasted code. Revisit if the project ever turns commercial |
| 37 | Encrypting the recordings on the laptop | not now: deferred indefinitely (owner, 2026-11-18) | deferred (owner) | encrypted folder (gocryptfs/age) now; full-drive encryption on the next drive | the owner chose to defer; revisit before any data leaves the laptop or a shared release |
| 38 | Sharing one headband with many programs | the recorder is the hub: it alone holds the Bluetooth link, records to disk, and serves a live stream (`/stream`, server-sent events) to any number of readers; a separate LSL bridge re-publishes it for standard tools | decided | let each program connect itself (impossible: one BLE link); LSL inside the recorder (pylsl + numpy next to the SDK risks the known segfault); WebSockets (needs a dependency) | recording never depends on a reader; readers cannot crash the recorder; the browser pages can move from polling `/state` (adds up to 2 s of delay) to the stream. Measured on the fake headband: 2 stream readers and an LSL reader got every batch, 250 samples/s, markers included |
| 39 | Smooth sound on a busy laptop or a phone (`sound.html`) | the audio thread does the gliding (`setTargetAtTime` once per new target, not 20 updates a second from a page timer); `latencyHint: 'playback'`; on phones a light mode (feedback-delay room instead of convolution reverb, no compressor, no pitch-drift LFOs: about a third of the audio work); the page counts dropouts (audio clock falling behind, `getOutputTimestamp`) and resumes audio the browser pauses | decided; latency hint revisited in row 45 | AudioWorklet synthesis; pre-rendered loops in an `<audio>` element | the bigger buffers add ~0.1 s, small next to today's ~2 s analysis delay; research A (pass 2) wants low delay for training, so revisit when the delay drops to ~0.1 s (stream + fast alpha) or if dropouts remain in light mode |
| 40 | Battery readout | the headband reports whole percent and sticks at 0-2 % / 100 % while streaming (seen 05:17-08:03, 2026-11-18): treat those as stuck, fit a line through the last 90 min of believable readings (earlier runs' logs included) and show "35% · about 6 h left" (`scripts/battery.py`) | decided | raw percent only | switch if the SDK or firmware gives a voltage or a reliable gauge |
| 41 | Brain sound input and timing | the page reads the recorder's `/stream` (server-sent events) instead of polling `/state`; the core is sample-driven: it keeps 8 s per channel, filters every sample and decides every 25 samples (0.1 s) of sample time, never by a clock; sources (live stream, demo, saved-session replay) sit behind one seam (`sound-source.js`); contact still comes from `/state` every 10 s; a skipped stream index, a pause over 1 s in the recorder's arrival times or a reconnect is a gap (filters restart, 1 s settle) | decided | keep polling `/state` (waits up to 0.25 s, then a 1 s window); decide on each arriving batch (the output would depend on batch timing, so no exact replay); WebSocket | polling plus a 1 s DFT made the sound ~2 s late; batches arrive every ~0.1 s, and sample-time steps make the core deterministic and port 1:1 to the phone app's own BrainBit callbacks. The recorder is unchanged. Switch to per-batch decisions only if 0.1 s steps prove too coarse |
| 42 | Fast alpha and muscle estimate | alpha: DC block, two equal band-pass stages (RBJ biquads, -3 dB at peak ± 2 Hz), envelope from the signal and its slope (exact for a sine at the centre), 0.05 s smoothing; half-way to a new level 0.1-0.2 s after the eyes close or open; theta the same at peak - 6 to peak - 2 Hz (from 3 Hz); muscle: 50 Hz notch + a 30-45 Hz pair, power smoothed 0.25 s | decided | short sliding Goertzel/DFT (0.25-0.5 s windows); Hilbert FIR; a learned forecaster (Semenkov 2023) | IIR filters cost almost nothing per sample and port 1:1 to Dart; a 4 Hz wide band has ~0.1 s delay by nature, and the envelope adds little. Switch to a forecaster only if the measured end-to-end delay stays over 0.3 s on wired output |
| 43 | Brain sound calibration and target line | silent calibration, 30 s eyes open + 60 s eyes closed, ending once 30 s are clean; alpha peak from the eyes-closed spectrum (7-13 Hz, 2x the median, else 8-12 Hz); line = 35th percentile of eyes-closed alpha, spread = robust SD (at least 0.1 log units); the line is held for 3-minute blocks, then moves 0.15 SD up after a block above it over 80 % of the counted time, down under 50 %; sound = S-curve of (alpha - line) / SD, flat beyond ±2; without calibration the first minute (eyes closed, neutral sound) is the baseline | decided | the old eyes-open = 0 / eyes-closed = 1 scale (row 18); a running percentile; a moving target that holds 80 % rewards | research A pass 2: eyes closed cleared the old 0.7 level nearly all the time, so bells carried no information; resetting the target every 30 s did no better than placebo (Lansbergen 2011), slow tightening did better (Dhindsa 2018). Carrying the line into the next session (if the new baseline is within 1 SD) waits for the meditation mode (row 21). Switch the percentile or step if real sessions sit far from 60-65 % above the line |
| 44 | Brain sound bells | a bell after at least 3 s above the line within the last 4 s, with no gate in those 4 s; then at least 5 s (training) or 15 s (relax) to the next, and at most 6 in any minute; the 4 s count restarts after each bell | decided | the old rule (0.7 for 3 s, then a 2 s lock; row 20); random rewards; no bells | the report's 3-6 a minute; a gap alone would allow 12 a minute in training. Very strong alpha gives 6 quick bells then a pause, and the block rule raises the line within 3 minutes. Switch to a longer training gap if that feels busy |
| 45 | Training and relax modes | training: `latencyHint: 'interactive'`, bed glides 0.25-0.3 s (warmth 1.5 s), 5 s bell gap; relax: `'playback'`, ~1 s glides, 15 s gap; the light engine is automatic on phones (the checkbox is gone: it read as a colour theme); the browser's reported base and output latency, dropouts and stream delay go into the session log and the stop marker | decided | one mode for everything; `'interactive'` everywhere | trade-off: small buffers save ~0.1 s but bring dropouts back on a busy laptop or phone, which is why row 39 chose `'playback'`. With the analysis now ~0.15 s, 0.1 s of buffer matters for training (0.24 s trained best, Belinskaia 2020) and not for relaxing. Classic Bluetooth adds 0.15-0.5 s either way, so training says "wired headphones or speaker". Switch training to `'balanced'` if dropouts return |
| 46 | Brain sound gates | muscle: 30-45 Hz at T3/T4 and at O1/O2, each pair against its own 20th percentile of the last 2 min, gate just over 2x rest; blink: slow wave (under 4 Hz) at O1/O2 over max(40 µV, 5x its usual size); drift to sleep: the shared `sleep-guard.js` (theta/alpha 1.5x baseline for 10 s, or the alpha peak 1 Hz slower over 10 s, or alpha under half the baseline in over half of 30 s); muscle and blink held 0.5 s; any gate: no bells, neutral bed (drowsy: a little brighter), not counted for the line | decided | the report's median + 3 MAD muscle rest; a 55-95 Hz muscle band; no blink gate in alpha mode | a median rest was dragged up by a 60 s clench in the tests and then missed the next jaw bursts; 30-45 Hz stays until the clench test picks the band. BrainBit's forehead reference carries blinks into O1/O2. The sleep guard is the ROADMAP's "one shared detector"; it cannot tell open eyes from dozing. All thresholds are planning values for the owner's bench tests |
| 47 | Brain sound session log | kept in memory: a line every 0.1 s (quality, gate, alpha against the line, calm, roughness, bell), calibration / block / bell / gap / contact events and the float32 input samples; `window.__sound.session()` returns it as JSON; markers only for start, calibration, each block, each bell and stop; `replaySession()` feeds the samples through a fresh core and must reproduce every line | decided | a `/feedback` endpoint in the recorder (needs a recorder change); logging only the control stream (cannot re-run the core); a download button (no: row 48) | exact replay tests the core and is the base of the replayed-feedback check (row 23), which will replay the control stream; about 2 MB per 30 min in memory. Switch to saving sessions to disk when the phone app stores them |
| 48 | The web pages | a stopgap until the Android app: logic in pure modules with Dart port notes at the top of each, minimal page wiring, no extra page features | decided (owner, 2026-11-18) | growing the pages into the product | the bet is on the phone app, soon |
| 49 | Phone recorder after a Bluetooth drop | save; leave the lost sensor object alone (the one call on a sensor that may be out of range is the state read, as on the laptop); then a new sensor object for the same headband, reached directly by its saved address and sensor info without a scan, through the scanner that found it; waits 2, 5, 10, 30, then every 60 s; every third try a 15 s scan instead; a new file set each time (`to_bdf.py` joins them). A `drop` line, every `reconnect try` and the `reconnected` line go into the `.log` with times. A direct connection stays marked in the saved night state until the new run's first data, so a process that dies in that stretch comes back (Android restarts it) scanning only | decided (assistant; changed 2026-11-18 from a fresh scan after every drop) | a fresh scan after every drop (the first pick); `connect()` again on the lost sensor object (the research note's idea; the laptop's in-process reconnect after a drop segfaulted); the laptop's disconnect / connect of the same in-range sensor after a stall (here a stall ends the run and the next run gets a new object) | Android stops unfiltered scans while the screen is off and the SDK's scans look unfiltered, so a 3 a.m. rescan may never see the headband (research 2026-11-18-07, phone speed and battery). Unknown until the phone test: whether the SDK makes a sensor without a fresh scan (its library has a "Sensor not founded" error) and whether a second sensor for the same address works while the lost one exists. Switch to scans only if test T3(d) shows direct tries failing or crashing; to `connect()` on the old object if both direct tries and screen-off scans fail |
| 50 | Phone recorder when an SDK call blocks | every SDK call has a deadline (30 s; scan + connect 90 s; direct connect 60 s); past it the data is saved, the foreground-task restart alarm is armed and the app process ends; Android restarts the service, which continues the night with a new file set | decided (assistant) | keep going in the same process; stop the night | the Android plugin runs commands under one lock, so one blocked call blocks all later ones; a fresh process is the laptop's proven cure. Switch if T3 shows HyperOS does not bring the service back |
| 51 | Contact quality words | good <= 1 MOhm, fair <= 2.5 MOhm, poor above, "none" when infinite or missing | planning assumption, unproven | BrainBit's own limits (README says "2.5 ohms", unit garbled) | no measured on-head values were available to the phone work; recalibrate from the owner's `contact ohms` log lines |
| 52 | When the phone checks contact | once a night, at the first connection; not after reconnects or process restarts | decided (assistant) | before every file set | on the laptop a stream started right after a resistance check once sent nothing; at 3 a.m. the data matter more than a contact reading |
| 53 | Where the phone writes | the app's private folder `files/recordings` (Context.getFilesDir), handed to both Flutter engines by a small Kotlin channel (registered in the service's engine through `FlutterForegroundTaskLifecycleListener` in `Application.onCreate`); nothing leaves it except by Export (row 56) | decided (assistant; changed 2026-11-18 from `Android/media`) | `Android/media/<app>/recordings` (the first pick: adb pull and Syncthing see it, but so can other apps with storage access); `Android/data/...` (still external storage); MediaStore | EEG identifies a person like a fingerprint and is health data; the private folder cannot be read by other apps and Android encrypts it (research 2026-11-18-07, app tech and data). Switch to a shared folder only for encrypted copies (age to the laptop's key) if automatic Syncthing sync becomes worth that work |
| 54 | Phone permissions | Nearby devices only on Android 12+ (scan flagged `neverForLocation`); location only on Android 11 and older; `ACCESS_BACKGROUND_LOCATION` from neurosdk2 removed; battery-optimisation exemption asked with the system dialog (GitHub build) | decided (assistant) | location on every version, as BrainBit's example does | fewer scary prompts. Switch if test T0 finds no headband without location |

| 851 | The judge on the live signal | per second and electrode (`SecondMaker`): **off** = moved under 1.4 µV or at the amplifier's limit, **wild** = over 500 µV peak to peak above 2 Hz, **loud** = over 250 µV, else calm (the detectors' limits, `Quality`, now the only set for a word on a screen). A second where at least three electrodes in four (and at least two) are loud or wild together is **busy**: the wearer or the shared forehead reference, never held against an electrode; the card says "busy" and "the band is on". | 1133 | Focus sessions: the rule as built, checked against the research (settles rows 510-512, "proposed" until now; the importer is built) | `scripts/aw_focus.py`, rule `focus-v1 min=35 switch=3`, from ActivityWatch's window and away-from-keyboard buckets on this laptop (`localhost:5600`, read through `break_hints.py`'s code); window titles read in memory only, never written. Time counts only while you are not away; away gaps under 3 minutes count as active time, gaps of 3 to under 5 minutes pause the clock but keep the session, 5 minutes or more is a real break. Windows are grouped into a family: **code** (terminals, editors, IDEs, AI-assistant windows, browser pages whose title names documentation or code hosting) or **write** (office, notes, PDFs); anything else is its own app and is not work (only `--any-app` counts it). Up to 3 minutes of active time away from the family in total between two stretches of it is allowed and belongs to the session; more ends it. A session is 35 active minutes or more; a longer run is still one session; it belongs to the 04:00-04:00 day and the 2-hour block it started in; counted and never summed as hours; a day under 35 active minutes has no line (no data is not zero); a run already going on when the range starts is not counted. The research (Google, Brown 2023): related actions together for 35-60 minutes, a quick related interruption does not break it, count sessions, never hours. Where it differs: Google breaks a session after 10 minutes without events and learns which actions belong together with a model over tool events; here the break is `break_hints.py`'s 5 minutes away and "related" is a word list of window names, because window names are all ActivityWatch has and titles are private. ## Untested things the plan rests on

1. An all-night Bluetooth recording on the Xiaomi phone (Xiaomi kills background apps).
2. Whether the headband is bearable to sleep in and keeps contact lying down.
3. Whether sleep scoring works at these electrode positions.

## Mission (user, 2026-11-18)

Affordable EEG for everyone: step-by-step guides to build a helmet at home (what to buy, how to assemble)
plus an open app, with outside contributors expected, that helps people understand themselves.
The owner's phone: Xiaomi 12T-family (Android; Xiaomi's system is known for killing background apps,
which matters for overnight recording).

## Sibling projects (user, 2026-11-18)

`../utof_tracker` (personal life tracker: Flutter app for Android and Linux plus Python analysis in `pc/`) and
`../pls_breathe` (Expo / React Native breathing app) share the same aim. Data from this app is meant to flow
into utof_tracker for deeper analysis, so a clean export / data contract is a requirement, not an extra.
utof_tracker is Flutter on Android (its Linux build is only a development harness with fake phone data); its pc/ side is Python. The phone there is recorded as a Xiaomi 11T, HyperOS, Android 14 (the owner said "12T or something"; confirm with `adb shell getprop ro.product.model`).

## Research (2026-11-18)

Eight topic notes in `_local/research/2026-11-18-01-*.md`; combined report
`_local/research/2026-11-18-01-eeg-life-tracker-architecture.md` (alternatives table, 17 risks with the
cheapest test for each, first-week build order). The picks below come from it.

Follow-ups (cloud session, merged 2026-11-18) in `docs/research/`: `anysleep.md`, `yasa-single-occipital.md`,
`neurosdk2-flutter.md`, `android-overnight-ble-xiaomi.md` (includes the phone settings checklist and test plan),
`health-connect.md`, `bdf-plus-writing.md`.

## Tools made so far

- `scripts/record_night.sh`: records the BrainBit to `_local/recordings/` until Ctrl+C, keeps the laptop awake,
  starts a fresh recorder after a stall (row 17). Serves a live check page on port 8765 (signal, hum, contact).
- `scripts/to_bdf.py`: joins one night's files into a BDF+ file with gap annotations and prints a quality summary.
- `scripts/brainbit_probe.py`: scan, connect, contact, 5 s of signal.
- `analysis/eeg_detectors.py` (with `validate_detectors.py` and `make_detector_fixture.py`) and `app/lib/detectors/`:
  the shared detectors and the drift-off detector, the same rules in Python and Dart ([docs/detectors.md](docs/detectors.md), rows 85-89).
- `scripts/brainbit_ble.py`, `scripts/record_ble.py`, `scripts/ble_probe.py`: our own Bluetooth driver, the recorder
  on it (`DRIVER=ble scripts/record_night.sh`) and its probe for the real headband (rows 90-92, docs/own-ble-driver.md).
- `scripts/score_sleep.py`: AnySleep (3 runs averaged, 4 channels, 0.3 Hz high-pass added by us) and YASA (O1-T3) per 30 s epoch;
  epochs missing over 5 s are not scored; writes JSON and a hypnogram PNG. Environment: `analysis/` (uv, CPU torch).
  A night over 14 h goes the phone's way (two passes with the app's ONNX models; rows 310-312).
- `app/`: the Android recorder (Flutter, branch `feat/android-recorder`, rows 49-57). Writes the same files as
  `record_night.py` to its private folder; "Export a night" copies a night out. Not yet run on a phone.
- `scripts/web/morning.html`: guided 5-minute morning check-in (contact, eyes closed, eyes open, ratings) and a
  2-minute setup test, served by the recorder; `?short=1` for 60 s phases.
- `scripts/morning_check.py`: analyses the newest check-in in a recording, writes `<start>.morning.json` and
  compares it with the last 14 (rows 18-24).
- `scripts/export_events.py`: `eeg_sleep_night` and `eeg_morning_check` events in utof_tracker's envelope (docs/data-format.md); a separate
- `scripts/break_hints.py`: soft break hints from ActivityWatch, `break_hint` events (docs/break-hints.md).
- `scripts/tray.py` + `scripts/laptop_work.py`: the laptop tray: work sessions found from ActivityWatch (ask first,
  automatic when sure, or by hand), breaks, the end after 30 min away, break hints on break_hints.py's rules, a file
  per session in `_local/laptop/work/` (rows 570-579, docs/laptop-tray.md).
- `scripts/sleep_report.py` + `scripts/night_files.py` + `scripts/web/sleep.html`: the automatic sleep report (row 82):
  the Sleep page starts the scoring through the hub and shows total sleep, sleep periods, the chart and the newest
  morning check-in result; morning.html works its result out after Save (`scripts/web/morning-result.js`).
- `scripts/export_events.py`: `eeg_sleep_night` events in utof_tracker's envelope (docs/data-format.md); a separate
  type from utof_tracker's own `sleep_night`, so neither overwrites the other. Since 2026-11-19 `--daily` (with
  `scripts/day_summary.py`) also writes one `eeg_day` per day and the measure list, and utof_tracker reads them
  (rows 550-559, `docs/utof-interface.md`).
- `scripts/import_phone.py`: the phone app's Export data folder onto the laptop, every file checked against the
  export's SHA-256 list; raw nights into `_local/recordings/`, the rest into `_local/phone/` (rows 150-157).
- `scripts/phone_link.py` (with `scripts/qr_code.py`, a standard-library QR encoder): connects the phone by one
  scan of the QR code it shows, then mirrors the phone's files and serves the live pages in the same window; the
  phone tells it where it is whenever Share on Wi-Fi is on (rows 530-539, `docs/phone-link.md`).
- `analysis/lucid_cues.py`, `analysis/lucid_signals.py`, `analysis/make_lucid_fixture.py`: the lucid dream cues' rule
  and dream-signal finder (references for `app/lib/lucid/`), their replay and false-signal count on a recorded night,
  and the Dart port's fixture (rows 300-309).
- `scripts/phone_night_check.py`: how a night recorded by the phone went, in one command: every gap with its
  reason, lost packets per hour, sample rate, drops and reconnects, restarts by Android, batteries, contact,
  signal sanity, the phone's sleep report, a verdict and the top 3 fixes; `--compare` puts a laptop night
  beside it (row 320).
- `analysis/two_pass.py`: the phone's two-pass sleep scorer in Python (the reference for `app/lib/sleep/model.dart`,
  rows 260-264); `score_sleep.py` uses it for nights over 14 h, and `two_pass.py <night.bdf>` compares a night's
  stages three ways against the laptop's report (rows 310-312).
- `scripts/catalog.py`: a catalog of every night on the laptop (laptop and phone nights: files, hours, missing
  minutes, the report's asleep / deep / dream minutes, check-in) with the SHA-256 of every file in
  `_local/catalog/`; says what grew, changed or went missing since the last run; `--check` re-hashes everything
  (rows 313-318).
- `scripts/pack_old.py` (daily) and `app/lib/recorder/packed.dart` (the phone, between nights): pack raw `.f32` files
  nothing has written for a day, losslessly and checked (`.f32.xz` on the laptop, `.f32.gz` on the phone); every
  reader opens either form through `scripts/packed.py` or `packed.dart` (rows 540-549).
- `scripts/diy_board.py`, `scripts/record_diy.py`: the laptop host for the DIY ADS1299 board (its "E9" frames over
  USB serial or Wi-Fi UDP): `DRIVER=diy scripts/record_night.sh` records it into `_local/recordings-diy/` with the
  same files and live pages; tested on a fake board (`tests/fake_diy/`) and on frames from the firmware's own code,
  not yet on the board (rows 350-359, [docs/diy-board.md](docs/diy-board.md)).
- `app/lib/headband/diy/`: the same board in the phone app over Wi-Fi (UDP 9000): a Dart port of the decoder pinned to
  the Python one by shared vectors, the board behind the app's headband interface, its night in
  `files/recordings-diy/`; "Headband: DIY board (test)" on the Headband connection page, off by default; tested on a
  fake board in Dart and a real UDP socket (rows 360-369, docs/diy-board.md "On the phone").
